{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-29.39801pt}{14.73767pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par\par\par
\lxSVG@closescope {}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=(-ie)^{2}\mu^{2\epsilon}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\ell^{d}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\gamma_{\mu}(\not{\ell}+\not{p}+m)\gamma^{\mu}}{[\ell^{2}-z][(\ell+p)^{2}-m^{2}]}\,,

(D.1)

where the green blob indicates the massive photon. The counterterms then read

\displaystyle\big(m\tilde{K}(\epsilon)\big)^{-1}\langle\delta m\rangle_{\ell}(z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{\epsilon}-6-\frac{1}{x}-x+(-3-4x-x^{2})H_{0}(x)+\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{x^{2}}H_{-1}(x)\,,

(D.2a)

\displaystyle\tilde{K}(\epsilon)^{-1}\langle\delta Z\rangle_{\ell}(z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\epsilon}-10-\frac{3}{x}-3x-\frac{1-x-9x^{2}-3x^{3}}{1-x}H_{0}(x)

(D.2b)

\displaystyle+(14+\frac{3}{x^{2}}+\frac{12}{x}+12x+3x^{2})H_{-1}(x)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{K}(\epsilon)=4\pi\alpha\Big(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\Big)K(\epsilon)

(D.3)

and
K(\epsilon)
defined in (
A.3
). We have expressed the result in terms of HPLs with the letter

\displaystyle x=\frac{\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{z-4m^{2}}}{\sqrt{z}+\sqrt{z-4m^{2}}}\,.

(D.4)

Since
0<z
in the dispersive integral (
3.5
), the
i\delta
prescription for
x
is irrelevant in this case.

Appendix E
Soft integrals

In (
5.46a
) and (
5.46b
) we have defined the two integrals necessary to
construct the soft contribution. These integrals
are universal and are given here in
d=4-2\epsilon
dimensions with
\mu
denoting the scale of dimensional regularisation. With

s_{ij}=2p_{i}\cdot p_{j}
and
s_{i\gamma}=2k\cdot p_{i}
we have

\displaystyle I_{1}(p_{i},k)

\displaystyle=i\mu^{2\epsilon}\int\frac{\text{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{1}{[\ell^{2}+i\delta][\ell\cdot p_{i}-k\cdot p_{i}+i\delta]}

(E.1a)

\displaystyle=-2K(\epsilon)\bigg(\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{s_{i\gamma}}+i\delta\bigg)^{2\epsilon-1}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m_{i}^{2}}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\Gamma(1-\epsilon)^{2}\Gamma(2\epsilon-1)\,,

(E.1b)

\displaystyle I_{2}(p_{i},p_{j},k)

\displaystyle=i\mu^{2\epsilon}\int\frac{\text{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{1}{[\ell^{2}+i\delta][-\ell\cdot p_{j}+i\delta][\ell\cdot p_{i}-k\cdot p_{i}+i\delta]}

(E.1c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\frac{8K(\epsilon)}{s_{ij}}\bigg(\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{s_{i\gamma}}+i\delta\bigg)^{2\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m_{i}^{2}}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{|s_{ij}|}{2m_{i}m_{j}}\bigg)^{2\epsilon}\Bigg\{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)^{2}\Gamma(2\epsilon-1)\\
&\quad\ \,\times{}_{2}F_{1}\biggl[\genfrac{.}{.}{0.0pt}{}{\tfrac 12-\epsilon,1-\epsilon}{\tfrac 32-\epsilon};v_{ij}^{2}\biggr]+i\pi\Big(-\frac{1}{4}+i\delta\Big)^{-\epsilon}v_{ij}^{-1+2\epsilon}\Gamma(1-2\epsilon)\Gamma(2\epsilon)\Theta(s_{ij})\Bigg\}\end{split}

(E.1d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\frac{4K(\epsilon)}{s_{ij}}\frac{1+\chi}{1-\chi}\bigg(\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{s_{i\gamma}}+i\delta\bigg)^{2\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m_{i}^{2}}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\Bigg\{\frac{H_{0}(\chi)}{2\epsilon}-\zeta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}H_{0,0}(\chi)\\
&\quad\ -H_{1,0}(\chi)+\Theta(s_{ij})6\zeta_{2}+i\pi\Theta(s_{ij})\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}-H_{0}(\chi)-2H_{1}(\chi)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\Bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(E.1e)

where
v_{ij}=\sqrt{1-4m_{i}^{2}m_{j}^{2}/s_{ij}^{2}}=(1-\chi)/(1+\chi)
and
K(\epsilon)
is defined in (
A.3
). Since
0<v_{ij}<1
and thus
0<\chi<1
all HPLs are
manifestly real.

Appendix F
Splitting functions

In the following we give the explicit expressions for all the
quantities that enter the collinear factorisation
formulas (
6.23
) (ISR) and (
6.25
)
(FSR). The results are presented in a form that can be used in three
major flavours of dimensional regularisation: the four-dimensional
helicity scheme (
fdh
), ’t Hooft-Veltman scheme (
hv
), and
conventional dimensional regularisation (
cdr
)
(see
[
84
]
and references therein for the
definitions of these schemes). To this end, we keep the dimensionality
of
\epsilon
scalars,
n_{\epsilon}
, explicit in the poles but set it to
zero in the finite parts. The regularisation-scheme dependence is
therefore manifest as terms
\propto n_{\epsilon}
. The corresponding
results in
hv
and
cdr
can be obtained by setting
n_{\epsilon}=0
.
Inserting
n_{\epsilon}=2\epsilon
, on the other hand, retrieves the
expressions in
fdh
. Furthermore, in the case of
hv
and
fdh

\epsilon
has to be set to zero in the tree-level splitting function.

In agreement with
[
2
]
the massification
constant reads

\displaystyle Z^{(1)}=4\pi\alpha K(\epsilon)\Big(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\Big)^{\epsilon}\Big(\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(1-\frac{1}{2}n_{\epsilon})+4+2\zeta_{2}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,,

(F.1)

with
\alpha=Q^{2}/(4\pi)=e^{2}/(4\pi)
and
K(\epsilon)
defined in (
A.3
).

We then define the invariant
s_{kp}=2k\cdot p
where the photon
momentum
k
is collinear to an initial- or final-state fermion
p
.
The initial-state collinear splitting function

\displaystyle J_{\text{ISR}}=J_{\text{ISR}}^{(0)}+J_{\text{ISR}}^{(1)}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{2})

(F.2)

can conveniently be written in terms of

\displaystyle x=\frac{E_{p}-E_{k}}{E_{p}},\qquad u=\frac{s_{kp}-m^{2}}{s_{kp}}\,,

(F.3)

as

\displaystyle J_{\text{ISR}}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi\alpha(1-u)}{m^{2}(1-x)x}\big(1-2x+3x^{2}+2xu-2x^{2}u-\epsilon(1-x)^{2}\big)\,,

(F.4a)

\displaystyle J_{\text{ISR}}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle 8\pi\alpha J_{\text{ISR}}^{(0)}K(\epsilon)\Big(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\Big)^{\epsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{4\epsilon}(2-8H_{0}(x)-n_{\epsilon})\Big)+\alpha^{2}\frac{4(1-u)}{xm^{2}}\tilde{J}_{\text{ISR}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,,

(F.4b)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{\text{ISR}}=&\frac{1}{u(1-x)}(-2u^{2}x^{2}+2u^{2}+2ux^{2}+2ux+x^{2}-x)\\
+&\frac{\zeta_{2}}{1-x}(2u^{2}x^{2}-4u^{2}x+2u^{2}-12ux^{2}+16ux-4u+13x^{2}-12x+5)\\
+&\frac{H_{1}(u)}{u^{2}}(2u^{3}x-5u^{2}x-2u^{3}+3u^{2}+2ux-u+x)\\
+&\frac{H_{1}(u)H_{0}(x)+H_{1,0}(x)}{1-x}(-8ux^{2}+8ux+10x^{2}-8x+2)\\
+&\big(H_{2}(u)+H_{1,1}(u)\big)(-2u^{2}x+2u^{2}+2ux-4u+2)\,.\end{split}

(F.5)

Because
0<x<1
and
u<1
the above expression is always real.

The result for the final-state splitting function

\displaystyle J_{\text{FSR}}=J_{\text{FSR}}^{(0)}+J_{\text{FSR}}^{(1)}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{3})

(F.6)

can be obtained from
J_{\text{ISR}}
via the crossing relation

p\to-p
. In particular, this implies
x\to z^{-1}
and
u\to v^{-1}
with

\displaystyle z=\frac{E_{p}}{E_{p}+E_{k}},\qquad v=\frac{s_{kp}}{m^{2}+s_{kp}}\,.

(F.7)

The corresponding analytic continuation is unambiguously defined via

s_{kp}\to s_{kp}+i\delta
or equivalently
u\to u-i\delta
. We then find

\displaystyle J_{\text{ISR}}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi\alpha(1-v)}{m^{2}v^{2}(1-z)}\big(vz^{2}-2vz+3v+2z-2-\epsilon v(1-z)^{2}\big)\,,

(F.8a)

\displaystyle J_{\text{FSR}}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle 8\pi\alpha J_{\text{FSR}}^{(0)}K(\epsilon)\Big(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\Big)^{\epsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{4\epsilon}(2+8H_{0}(z)-n_{\epsilon})\Big)+\alpha^{2}\frac{4(1-v)}{m^{2}v^{2}}\Big(\tilde{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{\text{Re}}+i\pi\tilde{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{\text{Im}}\ \Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,,

(F.8b)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{\text{Re}}=&\frac{1}{1-z}(-v^{2}z+v^{2}+2vz+2v+2z^{2}-2)\\
+&\frac{\zeta_{2}}{1-z}(-vz^{2}+6vz-7v-6z+6)\\
+&\big(H_{0}(v)+H_{1}(v)\big)(v^{2}z-v^{3}-2v^{2}-3vz+5v+2z-2)\\
+&\frac{H_{0}(v)H_{0}(z)+H_{1}(v)H_{0}(z)+H_{0,0}(z)+H_{1,0}(z)}{1-z}(-2vz^{2}+8vz-10v-8z+8)\\
+&\frac{H_{1,0}(v)+H_{1,1}(v)}{v}(-2v^{2}z+4vz-2v-2z+2)\,,\end{split}

(F.9a)

\displaystyle\vskip 2.84544pt\begin{split}\tilde{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{\text{Im}}=&-v^{2}z+v^{3}+2v^{2}+3vz-5v-2z+2\\
+&\frac{H_{0}(z)}{1-z}(2vz^{2}-8vz+10v+8z-8)\\
+&\frac{H_{1}(v)}{v}(2v^{2}z-4vz+2v+2z-2)\,.\end{split}

(F.9b)

The imaginary part is given explicitly leaving all of the HPLs real for the physical region where
0<z,u<1
. The massless version of the FSR splitting function entering (
6.27
) can be extracted from the spin-summed result of equations (II.10) and (II.11) in
[
133
]
by taking the QED limit. The corresponding expressions in the
fdh
scheme read

\displaystyle\bar{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{(0)}=\frac{8\pi\alpha}{s_{kp}}\frac{1+z^{2}}{1-z}\,,

(F.10a)

\displaystyle\bar{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{(1)}=16\pi\alpha K(\epsilon)\Big\{\bar{J}_{\text{FSR}}^{(0)}\Big(-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big(-\frac{\mu^{2}}{zs_{kp}}\Big)^{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big(-\frac{\mu^{2}}{s_{kp}}\Big)^{\epsilon}-H_{1,0}(z)-\zeta_{2}\Big)-\frac{4\pi\alpha}{s_{kp}}\Big\}\,.

(F.10b)
